56:["$","p","p-28",{"children":"The one thing I didn’t think Claude would be good at was matching the gameplay tempo to the song. A rhythm game is all about the feeling of the press landing exactly when the music hits, so I did this part manually for the default song, Extended, especially because I really wanted to nail the feeling of the game."}]
57:["$","p","p-29",{"children":"And of course Extended was the annoying one. It’s 120 BPM, but its kick doesn’t hit on every beat, it goes “DUM DUM, rest”, so pressing on every beat felt like every other press was falling on nothing. My first try was to play it at 60 BPM in bars of two beats, and 20 minutes later I threw that away and gave it a groove instead, a cycle of two beats with one step on the first beat and another one a dotted eighth later:"}]

65:["$","p","p-37",{"children":["The last piece is ",["$","code","code-0",{"children":"measurePhase"}],", the same one from the script that cuts the loops. The game runs it again in the browser every time it loads a song, because MP3 decoders add different amounts of silence at the start of the file, and without it every press would be off by however much silence your browser decided to add. It builds an envelope of how much louder the low end gets every millisecond, then tries every possible offset inside one beat and adds up the kicks that land on that offset, and on every beat after it. The offset with the most kick wins:"]}]

78:["$","p","p-49",{"children":"My games needed a splash screen, and a splash screen needs a logo, and I don’t have one, so I asked Gemini for something called GNAF, short for Games Nobody Asked For (I just needed it to look serious). What came back was a PNG, and the problem with a Gemini PNG is that it’s a PNG: blurry edges, a cyan and a lime that don’t exist anywhere else on my site, and zero chance of staying sharp on a 4K monitor. I cropped it down to 1198x603, just the letters and the tagline, and that crop is what Claude traced."}]

7a:["$","p","p-51",{"children":["The trace turned it into an SVG: the G and the tagline came out as curves, and the N, the A and the F came out as straight-edged polygons, because they are straight-edged polygons.
